(cdr env))\n            (error \"undefined\")\n            (env-lookup (cdr env) name)))))\n\n(define (env-define! env name value)\n  (set-car! env (cons (cons name value) (car env))))\n\n(define (env-set! env name value)\n  (let loop ((e env))\n    (let ((cell (assoc name (car e))))\n      (if cell\n          (set-cdr! cell value)\n          (if (not (null? (cdr e)))\n              (loop (cdr e))\n              (error \"undefined\"))))))\n```\n\nAnd to create a new frame:\n```scheme\n(define (extend-env params args env)\n  (define (make-frame p a)\n    (if (null? p)\n        '()\n        (cons (cons (car p) (car a)) (make-frame (cdr p) (cdr a)))))\n  (cons (make-frame params args) env))\n```\n\nWait, I also need to think about how the Python interpreter's global environment interacts with my Scheme evaluator's environment.\n\nMy Scheme evaluator needs access to the primitives. Let me now write the actual code.\n\nLet me think about a potential issue: when the metacircular evaluator evaluates `(define (f x) body)`, it needs to handle the function-definition shorthand. The frame is an association list: `((var1 . val1) (var2 . val2) ...)`.\n\n```scheme\n(define (extend-env vars vals env)\n  (prim-cons (pair-up vars vals) env))\n\n(define (pair-up vars vals)\n  (if (prim-null? vars)\n      '()\n      (prim-cons (prim-cons (prim-car vars) (prim-car vals))\n                (pair-up (prim-cdr vars) (prim-cdr vals)))))\n```\n\nFor `lookup-var`:\n```scheme\n(define (lookup-var name env)\n  (if (prim-null? env)\n      (error \"Unbound variable\")\n      (let ((frame (env-frame env)))\n        (let ((binding (assoc name frame)))\n          (if (prim-null? binding)\n              (lookup-var name (env-parent env))\n              (prim-cdr binding))))))\n```\n\nFor `assoc`:\n```scheme\n(define (assoc key alist)\n  (if (prim-null? alist)\n      '()\n      (if (prim-equal? key (prim-car (prim-car alist)))\n          (prim-car alist)\n          (assoc key (prim-cdr alist)))))\n```\n\nFor `define-var!`:\n```scheme\n(define (define-var! name val env)\n  (let ((frame (env-frame env)))\n    (let ((existing (assoc name frame)))\n      (if (prim-null? existing)\n          ;; Add new binding\n          (prim-set-car! env (prim-cons (prim-cons name val) frame))\n          ;; Update existing\n          (prim-set-cdr! existing val)))))\n```\n\nFor `set-var!`:\n```scheme\n(define (set-var! name val env)\n  (if (prim-null? env)\n      (error \"Unbound variable\")\n      (let ((frame (env-frame env)))\n        (let ((binding (assoc name frame)))\n          (if (prim-not (prim-null? binding))\n              (prim-set-cdr! binding val)\n              (set-var! name val (env-parent env)))))))\n```\n\nNow for the compound procedure and evaluator:\n\n```scheme\n;; Compound procedure representation: (cons 'procedure (cons params (cons body env)))\n(define (make-procedure params body env)\n  (prim-cons 'procedure (prim-cons params (prim-cons body env))))\n\n(define (compound-procedure? proc)\n  (and (pair? proc) (prim-eq? (prim-car proc) 'procedure)))\n\n(define (procedure-params proc) (prim-car (prim-cdr proc)))\n(define (procedure-body proc) (prim-car (prim-cdr (prim-cdr proc))))\n(define (procedure-env proc) (prim-cdr (prim-cdr (prim-cdr proc))))\n\n;; Primitive procedure representation: (cons 'primitive fn)\n(define (make-primitive fn)\n  (prim-cons 'primitive fn))\n\n(define (primitive? proc)\n  (and (pair? proc) (prim-eq? (prim-car proc) 'primitive)))\n\n(define (primitive-fn proc) (prim-cdr proc))\n\n(define (apply-primitive proc args)\n  (let ((fn (primitive-fn proc)))\n    (cond ((prim-null? args) (fn))\n          ((prim-null? (prim-cdr args)) (fn (prim-car args)))\n          ((prim-null? (prim-cdr (prim-cdr args))) (fn (prim-car args) (cadr args)))\n          ((prim-null? (prim-cdr (prim-cdr (prim-cdr args))))\n           (fn (prim-car args) (cadr args) (caddr args)))\n          (else (error \"Too many args for primitive\")))))\n```\n\nBut wait, `fn` in apply-primitive is a Python lambda. When we say `(fn (prim-car args))`, the outer evaluator (interp.py) evaluates this:\n1. Evaluates `fn` → gets the Python lambda\n2. Evaluates `(prim-car args)` → gets the first arg value\n3. Sees that `fn` is callable → calls `fn(val1)`\n\nYes, this works!\n\nNow for the main `eval` and `apply`:\n\n```scheme\n(define (eval expr env)\n  (cond ((prim-number? expr) expr)\n        ((prim-string? expr) expr)\n        ((prim-symbol? expr) (lookup-var expr env))\n        ((not (pair? expr)) expr)  ;; booleans, None, etc. - self-evaluating\n        (else\n         (let ((op (prim-car expr)))\n           (cond ((prim-eq? op 'quote) (prim-car (prim-cdr expr)))\n                 ((prim-eq? op 'if) (eval-if expr env))\n                 ((prim-eq? op 'define) (eval-define expr env))\n                 ((prim-eq? op 'set!) In SICP, the metacircular evaluator installs primitive procedures in the global environment. Let me just write a practical metacircular evaluator.\n\n```scheme\n;;; Meta-circular evaluator\n;;; Usage: echo -e 'program.scm\\ninput' | python3 interp.py eval.scm\n\n;; Read filename from stdin  \n(define *input-file* (read))\n\n;; ===== Environment operations =====\n;; An environment is (bindings . parent)\n;; bindings = ((name . value) ...)\n;; parent = environment or () for global\n\n(define (assoc key alist)\n  (cond ((null? alist) #f)\n        ((eq?
